Buy the one that actually shows up— the one that can keep them company, remember things, give reminders, and give you some peace of mind.</p><p>Because what your parents need isn’t necessarily a “robot.”</p><p>Most of the time, it’s a lot simpler than that.</p><p>They just want someone—or something—that remembers whether they took their meds today, how they’re feeling, and whether there’s someone to talk to at the end of the day.</p><p>And for those of us out here, busy with work and life… we’re not asking for much either.</p><p>We just want to feel a little more at ease when we can’t be there all the time.</p><p>A little less guilt. .quote-embed.font-size p, #article-num-0 .quote-embed .font-size p{font-size: 36px;}</style><div class="b6Cr_ article-body-container"><div class="fs-article fs-premium fs-responsive-text current-article font-body color-body bg-base font-accent article-subtype__masthead article-body premium-container"><figure class="embed-base image-embed embed-3" role="presentation"><div style="padding-top:59.49%;position:relative" class="image-embed__placeholder"><progressive-image class=" top-image" src="https://specials-images.forbesimg.com/imageserve/6985848970acb6c3378203b5/Ryan--the--wellness-robot--AI-companion-for-seniors--includes-an--activity-hub-/0x0.png?fit=scale" alt="Ryan, the &quot;wellness robot&quot; AI companion for seniors, includes an &quot;activity hub&quot; screen on its chest for playing games and following along with exercises." data-height="715" data-width="1200" fetchpriority="high" style="position:absolute;top:0"></progressive-image></div><div><div class="bMqrj"><div class="Jp--BF7-"><span style="-webkit-line-clamp:2" class="Ccg9Ib-7 _8XF2kHYM">Ryan, the &quot;wellness robot&quot; AI companion for seniors, includes an &quot;activity hub&quot; screen on its chest for playing games and following along with exercises.</span></div><small class="pGGCM2aD">DreamFace Technologies</small></div></div></figure><p>The idea sounds appealing. A virtual friend chatting with an older adult to keep him company after the death of his wife. For families worried about loneliness, an AI companion seems like a logical, 21st-century solution. </p><div style="min-height:0"><div class="G9049W9y _1apm68B5 L996f LfzXb inlineAdContainer" aria-hidden="true" role="presentation" tabindex="-1" data-id="ad-container" data-testid="ad-container"></div></div><h2 class="subhead-embed color-accent bg-base font-accent font-size text-align">Why Loneliness Is Dangerous For Seniors </h2><p>Loneliness among older adults is real and pervasive. About one-third of people age 50 to 80 feel lonely or isolated, according to 2024 findings from the <a href="https://www.sciencedaily.com/releases/2024/12/241209122559.htm?utm_source=chatgpt.com" rel="nofollow noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" class="color-link" title="https://www.sciencedaily.com/releases/2024/12/241209122559.htm?utm_source=chatgpt.com" data-ga-track="ExternalLink:https://www.sciencedaily.com/releases/2024/12/241209122559.htm?utm_source=chatgpt.com" aria-label="National Poll on Healthy Aging."><em data-ga-track="ExternalLink:https://www.sciencedaily.com/releases/2024/12/241209122559.htm?utm_source=chatgpt.com">National Poll on Healthy Aging</em>.</a> A 2025 <a href="https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11750934/" rel="nofollow noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" class="color-link" title="https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11750934/" data-ga-track="ExternalLink:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11750934/" aria-label="Aging Clinical and Experimental Research"><em data-ga-track="ExternalLink:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11750934/">Aging Clinical and Experimental Research</em></a><em> </em>study<em> </em>found that among older adults, social isolation increases the risk of death from any cause by 35%, living alone increases it by 21%, and loneliness increases the risk by 14%. </p><figure class="embed-base image-embed embed-16" role="presentation"><div style="padding-top:66.67%;position:relative" class="image-embed__placeholder"><progressive-image src="https://specials-images.forbesimg.com/imageserve/698585f8a401e5888dc0fffa/AI-companions-for-seniors-are-embedded-in-devices--apps--wearables-or-actual-robots-/0x0.jpg?fit=scale" alt="AI companions for seniors are embedded in devices, apps, wearables or actual robots. " data-height="1176" data-width="1764" fetchpriority="auto" style="position:absolute;top:0"></progressive-image></div><div><div class="bMqrj"><div class="Jp--BF7-"><span style="-webkit-line-clamp:2" class="Ccg9Ib-7 _8XF2kHYM">AI companions for seniors are embedded in devices, apps, wearables or actual robots.</span></div><small class="pGGCM2aD">getty</small></div></div></figure><p>To counter social isolation, AI companions promise on-demand connection and engagement, and can remind the elderly to take pills, hydrate and exercise. But can a voice powered by a large language model truly replicate human presence? </p><h2 class="subhead-embed color-accent bg-base font-accent font-size text-align">What Is An AI Companion? </h2><p><a href="https://www.forbes.com/sites/rashishrivastava/2025/10/18/lonely-seniors-are-turning-to-ai-bots-for-companionship/" target="_self" class="color-link" title="https://www.forbes.com/sites/rashishrivastava/2025/10/18/lonely-seniors-are-turning-to-ai-bots-for-companionship/" data-ga-track="InternalLink:https://www.forbes.com/sites/rashishrivastava/2025/10/18/lonely-seniors-are-turning-to-ai-bots-for-companionship/" aria-label="AI companions">AI companions</a> are software-driven personalities embedded in devices, apps, wearables or actual robots. The digital companions are built to interact with users in ways that feel natural and engaging, drawing on memories of previous interactions.</p><div style="min-height:0"><div class="G9049W9y _1apm68B5 L996f lj8Sr inlineAdContainer" aria-hidden="true" role="presentation" tabindex="-1" data-id="ad-container" data-testid="ad-container"></div></div><div class="article_paragraph_7"></div><div class="embed-base embedly-align"><fbs-embedly iframe-src="https://embedly.forbes.com/widgets/media.html?type=text%2Fhtml&amp;key=3ce26dc7e3454db5820ba084d28b4935&amp;schema=twitter&amp;url=https%3A//x.com/JuanCereigido/status/1958559652758814794&amp;image=" style="padding-bottom:56.2%"></fbs-embedly></div><div class="MWrh7"><script id="article-0-connatix-discovery-0" type="text/javascript">

</script></div><p>Research suggests that such empathic conversations may help reduce loneliness and cognitive decline.</p><p>Some AI models track vital signs and detect emergencies, including falls. Via sensors, many can detect presence (when someone enters a room). Most models coordinate data that’s collected on seniors with caregivers and families.</p><figure class="embed-base image-embed embed-42" role="presentation"><div style="padding-top:66.53%;position:relative" class="image-embed__placeholder"><progressive-image src="https://specials-images.forbesimg.com/imageserve/69858869a401e5888dc0fffc/An-assisted-living-service-robot-plays-a-card-game-with-a-resident-/1960x0.jpg?fit=scale" alt="An assisted living service robot plays a card game with a resident. " data-height="1754" data-width="2631" fetchpriority="auto" style="position:absolute;top:0"></progressive-image></div><div><div class="bMqrj"><div class="Jp--BF7-"><span style="-webkit-line-clamp:2" class="Ccg9Ib-7 _8XF2kHYM">An assisted living service robot plays a card game with a resident.</span></div><small class="pGGCM2aD">getty</small></div></div></figure><p>While the programs can supplement care and make life easier, such “pals” are tools, and can’t substitute for the nuanced, emotional connections humans provide. </p><h2 class="subhead-embed color-accent bg-base font-accent font-size text-align">Setting Boundaries: Safe Use For Older Adults</h2><div style="min-height:0"><div class="G9049W9y _1apm68B5 L996f lj8Sr inlineAdContainer" aria-hidden="true" role="presentation" tabindex="-1" data-id="ad-container" data-testid="ad-container"></div></div><p>Edward Morris knows the nuances of what such companions can offer. He teaches the elderly how to use generative AI tools such as ChatGPT, Gemini and Grok.</p><p>“The elderly first start using AI more like a ‘toy’ or something to enjoy, almost like a party trick,” said Morris, CEO and lead prompt engineer at UK-based <a href="https://www.Enigmatica.co.uk" rel="nofollow noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" class="color-link" title="https://www.Enigmatica.co.uk" data-ga-track="ExternalLink:https://www.enigmatica.co.uk/" aria-label="Enigmatica">Enigmatica</a>, which specializes in AI and prompt engineering. “In old age, there are many things the elderly miss. AI can help them reflect and reminisce. It can also help them keep up to date with things without being judged for being old.”</p><div class="embed-base embedly-align tiktok-align"><fbs-embedly iframe-src="https://embedly.forbes.com/widgets/media.html?src=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.tiktok.com%2Fembed%2Fv2%2F7587889208031743246&amp;display_name=tiktok&amp;url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.tiktok.com%2F%40yourfuturemachine%2Fvideo%2F7587889208031743246%3Fq%3Dai%2520companion%2520elderly%26t%3D1770359012359&amp;image=https%3A%2F%2Fp19-common-sign.tiktokcdn-us.com%2Ftos-useast5-p-0068-tx%2Focy42EBBvAmgoQ0BAaUAIE9kyANZiiUKIPBSn%7Etplv-tiktokx-origin.image%3Fdr%3D9636%26x-expires%3D1770530400%26x-signature%3DeI7Rp4jg3NilFCJJx0E%252Br%252FS4vnM%253D%26t%3D4d5b0474%26ps%3D13740610%26shp%3D81f88b70%26shcp%3D43f4a2f9%26idc%3Duseast5&amp;type=text%2Fhtml&amp;schema=tiktok" style="padding-bottom:205.88%"></fbs-embedly></div><p>Morris advises caregivers to use ChatGPT or a similar app for seniors who want a simple text and voice option.</p><p>He urges caregivers to add clear guardrails in the program’s custom instructions, spelling out boundaries that AI should never cross. They should include, among others, to never give medical diagnoses or medication advice, and not to present as the user’s only source of support.</p><p>Including such prompts gives AI “a personality and philosophy to live on,” Morris said. “That way, if your senior says something wrong, AI won&#x27;t agree with them immediately.”</p><div style="min-height:0"><div class="G9049W9y _1apm68B5 L996f lj8Sr inlineAdContainer" aria-hidden="true" role="presentation" tabindex="-1" data-id="ad-container" data-testid="ad-container"></div></div><div class="embed-base embedly-align tiktok-align"><fbs-embedly iframe-src="https://embedly.forbes.com/widgets/media.html?src=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.tiktok.com%2Fembed%2Fv2%2F7538304333671107871&amp;display_name=tiktok&amp;url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.tiktok.com%2F%40kforkhaos%2Fvideo%2F7538304333671107871%3Fis_from_webapp%3D1%26sender_device%3Dpc%26web_id%3D7588358209515046455&amp;image=https%3A%2F%2Fp16-common-sign.tiktokcdn-us.com%2Ftos-useast8-p-0068-tx2%2FoQmQAE6BjfoIRA3f69qOktfG5E3DICFRqJIIMN%7Etplv-tiktokx-origin.image%3Fdr%3D9636%26x-expires%3D1770530400%26x-signature%3D%252FVVat5shV419CRp8RaZW0ilQuc8%253D%26t%3D4d5b0474%26ps%3D13740610%26shp%3D81f88b70%26shcp%3D43f4a2f9%26idc%3Duseast8&amp;type=text%2Fhtml&amp;schema=tiktok" style="padding-bottom:205.88%"></fbs-embedly></div><p>Morris said to steer clear of standalone apps that offer companionship, which are often geared toward dating. Other AI models, however, are built specifically for seniors. </p><h2 class="subhead-embed color-accent bg-base font-accent font-size text-align">Seniors Should Have Realistic Expectations</h2><p>Seniors should understand what AI companions can and cannot do, which can help them avoid overrelying on the technology. Overreliance, in fact, should be monitored. “It’s almost understandable at an old age, but it can be incredibly unhealthy and border on addiction,” Morris said. </p><p>For families hoping AI will keep grandpa company, the biggest challenge isn’t technology, but psychology. </p><div class="embed-base embedly-align"><fbs-embedly iframe-src="https://embedly.forbes.com/widgets/media.html?type=text%2Fhtml&amp;key=3ce26dc7e3454db5820ba084d28b4935&amp;schema=twitter&amp;url=https%3A//x.com/JuanCereigido/status/1942616457285918768&amp;image=" style="padding-bottom:56.2%"></fbs-embedly></div><div style="min-height:0"><div class="G9049W9y _1apm68B5 L996f lj8Sr inlineAdContainer" aria-hidden="true" role="presentation" tabindex="-1" data-id="ad-container" data-testid="ad-container"></div></div><p>AI is known for being sycophantic, which can sometimes be useful for emotional support. “But excessive use can detach a person from reality, especially when they lose contact with other humans because of overreliance,” said <a href="https://phi.ntt-research.com/phi-people/tanaka-profile/" rel="nofollow noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" class="color-link" title="https://phi.ntt-research.com/phi-people/tanaka-profile/" data-ga-track="ExternalLink:https://phi.ntt-research.com/phi-people/tanaka-profile/" aria-label="Dr. Hidenori Tanaka">Dr. Hidenori Tanaka</a>, who leads the AI research initiative for Harvard University/NTT Research&#x27;s Brain Science Center.</p><p>Tanaka said those with dementia or who are vulnerable to delusions are especially susceptible. “It may unintentionally reinforce confusion or false beliefs,” he said. </p><h2 class="subhead-embed color-accent bg-base font-accent font-size text-align">Standalone AI Companions </h2><p>ElliQ is an AI-powered “proactive care companion” first introduced as a prototype in 2017 that was commercially launched in 2022.</p><div class="embed-base embedly-align"><fbs-embedly iframe-src="https://embedly.forbes.com/widgets/media.html?src=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.youtube.com%2Fembed%2Fz5X5-t0_fRA%3Ffeature%3Doembed&amp;display_name=YouTube&amp;url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.youtube.com%2Fshorts%2Fz5X5-t0_fRA&amp;image=https%3A%2F%2Fi.ytimg.com%2Fvi%2Fz5X5-t0_fRA%2Fhq2.jpg&amp;type=text%2Fhtml&amp;schema=youtube" style="padding-bottom:75%"></fbs-embedly></div><div style="min-height:0"><div class="G9049W9y _1apm68B5 L996f lj8Sr inlineAdContainer" aria-hidden="true" role="presentation" tabindex="-1" data-id="ad-container" data-testid="ad-container"></div></div><p>The head-shaped device tilts, swivels and lights up when communicating—its light ring helping to give it a sense of personality. A detachable screen sits on a cradle next to it, displaying content like news, video calls and photos. The device’s female voice, which is a bit robotic, tells jokes, plays music and games, creates AI-generated art, relays news and prompts users to take medications, among other tasks. Cameras and sensors recognize faces and enable ElliQ to interact more comprehensively. </p><div class="embed-base embedly-align"><fbs-embedly iframe-src="https://embedly.forbes.com/widgets/media.html?src=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.youtube.com%2Fembed%2FUex5yVKUFK0%3Fstart%3D4%26feature%3Doembed%26start%3D4&amp;display_name=YouTube&amp;url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.youtube.com%2Fwatch%3Fv%3DUex5yVKUFK0&amp;image=https%3A%2F%2Fi.ytimg.com%2Fvi%2FUex5yVKUFK0%2Fhqdefault.jpg&amp;type=text%2Fhtml&amp;schema=youtube" style="padding-bottom:56.21%"></fbs-embedly></div><p>Unlike ElliQ, <a href="https://www.dreamfacetech.com/" rel="nofollow noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" class="color-link" title="https://www.dreamfacetech.com/" data-ga-track="ExternalLink:https://www.dreamfacetech.com/" aria-label="Ryan, the “wellness robot,">Ryan, the “wellness robot,</a>” is human-sized with arms and a flared pedestal base that enables it to navigate. Its human-like animated face, which can make eye contact, appears on an oval screen on its head.</p><figure class="embed-base image-embed embed-5" role="presentation"><div style="padding-top:56.12%;position:relative" class="image-embed__placeholder"><progressive-image src="https://specials-images.forbesimg.com/imageserve/698584caa401e5888dc0fff8/Ryan--an-AI-companion-for-seniors--has-a-human-like-animated-face--which-can-make-eye/0x0.png?fit=scale" alt="Ryan, an AI companion for seniors, has a human-like animated face, which can make eye contact." data-height="675" data-width="1200" fetchpriority="auto" style="position:absolute;top:0"></progressive-image></div><div><div class="bMqrj"><div class="Jp--BF7-"><span style="-webkit-line-clamp:2" class="Ccg9Ib-7 _8XF2kHYM">Ryan, an AI companion for seniors, has a human-like animated face, which can make eye contact.</span></div><small class="pGGCM2aD">DreamFace Technologies</small></div></div></figure><p>A tablet-style screen on the robot’s chest acts as an “activity hub,” displaying games and exercise guides. Caregivers can access the screen to track a senior’s emotional patterns, cognitive performance, and overall mood. </p><div style="min-height:0"><div class="G9049W9y _1apm68B5 L996f lj8Sr inlineAdContainer" aria-hidden="true" role="presentation" tabindex="-1" data-id="ad-container" data-testid="ad-container"></div></div><p>Mohammad H. Mahoor helped develop Ryan with DreamFace Technologies at the University of Denver. “The aging population and ongoing caregiver shortages are pushing families and senior living communities to look for new ways to support overall wellness,” said Mahoor, a professor of computer science at the University of Denver.</p><h2 class="subhead-embed color-accent bg-base font-accent font-size text-align">Look For A Strict Privacy Policy </h2><div class="embed-base embedly-align tiktok-align"><fbs-embedly iframe-src="https://embedly.forbes.com/widgets/media.html?src=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.tiktok.com%2Fembed%2Fv2%2F7520690016910069005&amp;display_name=tiktok&amp;url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.tiktok.com%2F%40m8wellness%2Fvideo%2F7520690016910069005%3Fq%3Dai%2520companion%2520elderly%26t%3D1770359012359&amp;image=https%3A%2F%2Fp19-common-sign.tiktokcdn-us.com%2Ftos-useast5-p-0068-tx%2Fo8429FWko6DvwdreB6FEguD3EXAR2nPQQ6foSt%7Etplv-tiktokx-origin.image%3Fdr%3D9636%26x-expires%3D1770530400%26x-signature%3D5Ycl1Dz7nLH4l8ljIt9wv%252BghDro%253D%26t%3D4d5b0474%26ps%3D13740610%26shp%3D81f88b70%26shcp%3D43f4a2f9%26idc%3Duseast5&amp;type=text%2Fhtml&amp;schema=tiktok" style="padding-bottom:205.88%"></fbs-embedly></div><p>Mahoor advises to look for systems that are easy to use, designed specifically for older adults, and are “validated through real-world deployments—not just demos,” he said. The most reputable models will be backed by input from clinicians, caregivers and researchers, and tested in care settings.</p><p>Any company offering AI companions should have a strict privacy policy that controls how private information is handled. It should cover all information that is shared with AI, not just medical issues.</p><figure class="embed-base image-embed embed-58" role="presentation"><div style="padding-top:66.39%;position:relative" class="image-embed__placeholder"><progressive-image src="https://specials-images.forbesimg.com/imageserve/69858a4acb327b88b9f017c6/-The-elderly-first-start-using-AI-more-like-a-toy---said-Morris--CEO-and-lead-prompt/1960x0.jpg?fit=scale" alt="“The elderly first start using AI more like a toy,&quot; said Morris, CEO and lead prompt engineer at UK-based Enigmatica, which specializes in AI and prompt engineering. " data-height="1705" data-width="2563" fetchpriority="auto" style="position:absolute;top:0"></progressive-image></div><div><div class="bMqrj"><div class="Jp--BF7-"><span style="-webkit-line-clamp:2" class="Ccg9Ib-7 _8XF2kHYM">“The elderly first start using AI more like a toy,&quot; said Morris, CEO and lead prompt engineer at UK-based Enigmatica, which specializes in AI and prompt engineering.</span></div><small class="pGGCM2aD">dpa/picture alliance via Getty Images</small></div></div></figure><div style="min-height:0"><div class="G9049W9y _1apm68B5 L996f lj8Sr inlineAdContainer" aria-hidden="true" role="presentation" tabindex="-1" data-id="ad-container" data-testid="ad-container"></div></div><p>Ask a vendor how content—photos, video, messages—is stored. Consumer privacy advocate <a href="https://pixelprivacy.com/" rel="nofollow noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" class="color-link" title="https://pixelprivacy.com/" data-ga-track="ExternalLink:https://pixelprivacy.com/" aria-label="Chris Hauk">Chris Hauk</a> advises asking these questions: “Is the content only on the device, or on servers? If it is stored on a server, how long is it kept? Who is it shared with? Does the AI firm use the person&#x27;s interaction with the AI to train future versions?&quot;</p></div><div class="_38K2d"><a class="TYbQt color-body" href="https://www.forbes.com/sites/forbesstaff/article/forbes-editorial-values-and-standards/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Editorial Standards</a><span class="HMT72"></span><a class="TYbQt color-body" href="https://www.parsintl.com/publications/forbes/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Reprints &amp; Permissions</a></div></div></div></div><div data-testid="ad-container"><div class="fbs-ad--medianet fbs-ad--media_net_1_article_0-wrapper-nextjs" data-position="media_net_1_article_0" data-size="800x200" data-tagid="846910971" aria-hidden="true" role="presentation" tabindex="-1"></div></div></div></div></div><div class="paywall_ribbon P75Aa" style="margin-bottom:0"></div></main></article></div><footer class="smP7p--o"
